I don’t think it’s that clear in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I just get stuck in the moment. At the register I’ll tap the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll sort of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer even though the customer is right there waiting. Not like an image or a thought that keeps forcing itself in, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I realize I’m doing the tapping, or I’m paused there, and then I feel awkward because I know the customer is waiting. But I wouldn’t say there’s some specific unwanted thought repeating in my head. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uncomfortable in situations like work because you're worried that others might notice your behavior and judge you for it?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. A little, yeah… I wouldn’t say anxious in a big dramatic way, but I do get kind of stuck if I can’t do it the usual way.

Like at the register, I’ll tap the scanner in a certain pattern, or I’ll pause when I really should just be moving on, and if I try to stop myself it feels… uncomfortable, I guess. Then I might freeze for a second with my hand over the cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting. It’s embarrassing more than anything.

I know it doesn’t really fit the moment, and I’m trying not to make a problem of it, but last week it did get me written up, so… I guess it’s noticeable enough. Is that the kind of thing you mean?

I do get embarrassed if someone’s watching me just stand there or do something odd, because, you know, I don’t want to seem incompetent or strange.\n\nBut I’m not constantly thinking, “everyone’s judging me,” exactly. It’s more after I notice it happened, then I feel awkward about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, like tapping the scanner, have some kind of special meaning or connection that other people wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a special meaning kind of way. It’s not like I believe the scanner is sending me a message or anything like that.\n\nI mean, sometimes I notice a pattern after I’m doing it, and I feel embarrassed because it probably looks odd. But I wouldn’t say it has some hidden connection that other people wouldn’t understand. At least… I haven’t thought of it that way. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you felt like something was giving you a sign or message, even if it didn’t make logical sense, like something you saw or heard had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like something on the TV or a sound was meant just for me or anything. I’d probably remember if it felt that strong.\n\nSometimes I might notice coincidences or patterns, like anyone does, but I don’t really take them as messages. At least, I don’t think I do. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or distressed if you’re not able to do those tapping patterns or pause the way you usually do at the register?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I wouldn’t say anxious in a big dramatic way, but I do get kind of stuck if I can’t do it the usual way.\n\nLike at the register, I’ll tap the scanner in a certain pattern, or I’ll pause when I really should just be moving on, and if I try to stop myself it feels… uncomfortable, I guess. Then I might freeze for a second with my hand over the cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting. It’s embarrassing more than anything.\n\nI know it doesn’t really fit the moment, and I’m trying not to make a problem of it, but last week it did get me written up, so… I guess it’s noticeable enough. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before you tap the scanner or pause the way you usually do, do you feel a kind of urge or physical discomfort that makes you feel like you have to do it that way?\n\nPatient: It’s not exactly pain or anything physical, no. It’s more like I get caught in it before I’ve really decided to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the register, I’ll start tapping the scanner in that pattern, or I’ll sort of pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I should just keep moving. I know it doesn’t fit the moment, especially with a customer waiting, and I don’t want to make it a big deal… but I do feel stuck for a second. Last week that was part of why I got written up, so I guess it’s become more noticeable than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, brief sounds or movements that you couldn't control, like blinking your eyes, shrugging your shoulders, or making a noise without meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I blink like anyone does, but not in a way I’d call sudden or uncontrollable.\n\nI don’t really make noises without meaning to, or shrug or anything like that. At least nothing I’ve noticed or that anyone’s pointed out to me. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t know if I’d call it a strange sensation exactly. It’s more like I get… stuck in the moment.

Like at the register, I’ll start tapping the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I really should be finishing the transaction. It doesn’t really fit what’s happening, I know that. The customer is waiting, and I’m aware of it, but for a second I just don’t move on the way I’m supposed to.

I came in because it’s started causing problems at work. Last week I got written up for it, so… I guess it’s more than just a little habit now. Does that answer what you mean?

I don’t think it’s that clear in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I just get stuck in the moment. I don’t know that it feels like pressure exactly. It’s not like I’m in pain or anything. It’s more like… I get caught on it, if that makes sense.

I try not to make a big deal of it, but last week I got written up for it, so… I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself doing these repeated actions at work, like tapping the scanner or freezing, do you feel like you have to do them in a certain way to prevent something bad from happening or because you can’t stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… if I don’t tap it, something terrible will happen. I don’t think it’s that clear in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I just get stuck in the moment. At the register I’ll tap the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll sort of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer even though the customer is right there waiting. I know it doesn’t really fit what’s happening, and I try to play it off, but it’s been noticeable enough that I got written up last week. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself tapping the scanner or freezing at the register, are you having thoughts that come into your mind that you don’t want there, or that bother you, like an idea or image that keeps repeating?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like an image or a thought that keeps forcing itself in, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I realize I’m doing the tapping, or I’m paused there, and then I feel awkward because I know the customer is waiting. But I wouldn’t say there’s some specific unwanted thought repeating in my head. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uncomfortable in situations like work because you're worried that others might notice your behavior and judge you for it?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. I do get embarrassed if someone’s watching me just stand there or do something odd, because, you know, I don’t want to seem incompetent or strange.\n\nBut I’m not constantly thinking, “everyone’s judging me,” exactly. It’s more after I notice it happened, then I feel awkward about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, like tapping the scanner, have some kind of special meaning or connection that other people wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a special meaning kind of way. It’s not like I believe the scanner is sending me a message or anything like that.\n\nI mean, sometimes I notice a pattern after I’m doing it, and I feel embarrassed because it probably looks odd. I don’t think it’s to stop something bad from happening, exactly. It’s not like I believe something terrible will happen if I don’t tap the scanner the right number of times.

I guess I’m trying to make it look normal, but it’s been enough of a problem at the register that I got written up last week.

I’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s the kind of “just right” feeling you mean.

I try not to make a big deal of it, but last week I got written up for it, so… I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself doing these repeated actions at work, like tapping the scanner or freezing, do you feel like you have to do them in a certain way to prevent something bad from happening or because you can’t stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… if I don’t tap it, something terrible will happen. At least nothing I’ve noticed or that anyone’s pointed out to me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you ever feel a strange sensation or inner pressure that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a strange sensation exactly. Last week I got written up for it, so… I guess it’s more than just a little habit now. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, brief movements or sounds that you couldn’t control, like eye blinking, nose twitching, or making a noise without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not like blinking or twitching or making sounds without meaning to.\n\nI mean, if I do something like that, I haven’t noticed it, and nobody’s really told me I do. I hope I’m answering that the right way—it’s just not something I recognize in myself.\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you feel any kind of inner pressure, discomfort, or need to do it a certain way before you can move on?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it feels like pressure exactly. It’s not like I’m in pain or anything. It’s more like… I get caught on it, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the register I’ll start tapping the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll just kind of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer, even though the customer is standing there waiting. I try to make it seem like I’m just checking something, but it’s been noticeable enough that I got written up last week.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s the kind of “need” you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that you have to perform these tapping patterns or pauses in a specific way to prevent something from happening or to make sure things feel 'just right'?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s to stop something bad from happening, exactly. It’s not like I believe something terrible will happen if I don’t tap the scanner the right number of times.\n\nIt’s more that I get stuck doing it, or I pause when I should be moving on, like with my hand over the cash drawer. I don’t think it’s that clear in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I just get stuck in the moment. At the register I’ll tap the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll sort of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer even though the customer is right there waiting. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or distressed if you’re not able to do those tapping patterns or pause the way you usually do at the register?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I wouldn’t say anxious in a big dramatic way, but I do get kind of stuck if I can’t do it the usual way.\n\nLike at the register, I’ll tap the scanner in a certain pattern, or I’ll pause when I really should just be moving on, and if I try to stop myself it feels… uncomfortable, I guess. Then I might freeze for a second with my hand over the cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting. It’s embarrassing more than anything.\n\nI know it doesn’t really fit the moment, and I’m trying not to make a problem of it, but last week it did get me written up, so… I guess it’s noticeable enough. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before you tap the scanner or pause the way you usually do, do you feel a kind of urge or physical discomfort that makes you feel like you have to do it that way?\n\nPatient: It’s not exactly pain or anything physical, no. It’s more like I get caught in it before I’ve really decided to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the register, I’ll start tapping the scanner in that pattern, or I’ll sort of pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I should just keep moving. I know it doesn’t fit the moment, especially with a customer waiting, and I don’t want to make it a big deal… but I do feel stuck for a second. Last week that was part of why I got written up, so I guess it’s become more noticeable than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, brief sounds or movements that you couldn't control, like blinking your eyes, shrugging your shoulders, or making a noise without meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I don’t know if I’d call it a build-up, exactly. It’s not like a physical urge that comes on first.

I try not to make a big deal of it, but last week I got written up for it, so… I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself doing these repeated actions at work, like tapping the scanner or freezing, do you feel like you have to do them in a certain way to prevent something bad from happening or because you can’t stop thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… if I don’t tap it, something terrible will happen. I don’t think it’s that clear in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I just get stuck in the moment. At the register I’ll tap the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll sort of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer even though the customer is right there waiting. I know it doesn’t really fit what’s happening, and I try to play it off, but it’s been noticeable enough that I got written up last week. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself tapping the scanner or freezing at the register, are you having thoughts that come into your mind that you don’t want there, or that bother you, like an idea or image that keeps repeating?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like an image or a thought that keeps forcing itself in, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I realize I’m doing the tapping, or I’m paused there, and then I feel awkward because I know the customer is waiting. But I wouldn’t say there’s some specific unwanted thought repeating in my head. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uncomfortable in situations like work because you're worried that others might notice your behavior and judge you for it?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. I do get embarrassed if someone’s watching me just stand there or do something odd, because, you know, I don’t want to seem incompetent or strange.\n\nBut I’m not constantly thinking, “everyone’s judging me,” exactly. It’s more after I notice it happened, then I feel awkward about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your actions, like tapping the scanner, have some kind of special meaning or connection that other people wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a special meaning kind of way. It’s not like I believe the scanner is sending me a message or anything like that.\n\nI mean, sometimes I notice a pattern after I’m doing it, and I feel embarrassed because it probably looks odd. But I wouldn’t say it has some hidden connection that other people wouldn’t understand. At least… I haven’t thought of it that way. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you felt like something was giving you a sign or message, even if it didn’t make logical sense, like something you saw or heard had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like something on the TV or a sound was meant just for me or anything. I’d probably remember if it felt that strong.\n\nSometimes I might notice coincidences or patterns, like anyone does, but I don’t really take them as messages. At least, I don’t think I do. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or distressed if you’re not able to do those tapping patterns or pause the way you usually do at the register?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I wouldn’t say anxious in a big dramatic way, but I do get kind of stuck if I can’t do it the usual way.\n\nLike at the register, I’ll tap the scanner in a certain pattern, or I’ll pause when I really should just be moving on, and if I try to stop myself it feels… uncomfortable, I guess. Then I might freeze for a second with my hand over the cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting. It’s embarrassing more than anything.\n\nI know it doesn’t really fit the moment, and I’m trying not to make a problem of it, but last week it did get me written up, so… I guess it’s noticeable enough. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before you tap the scanner or pause the way you usually do, do you feel a kind of urge or physical discomfort that makes you feel like you have to do it that way?\n\nPatient: It’s not exactly pain or anything physical, no. It’s more like I get caught in it before I’ve really decided to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the register, I’ll start tapping the scanner in that pattern, or I’ll sort of pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I should just keep moving. I know it doesn’t fit the moment, especially with a customer waiting, and I don’t want to make it a big deal… but I do feel stuck for a second. Last week that was part of why I got written up, so I guess it’s become more noticeable than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, brief sounds or movements that you couldn't control, like blinking your eyes, shrugging your shoulders, or making a noise without meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I blink like anyone does, but not in a way I’d call sudden or uncontrollable.\n\nI don’t really make noises without meaning to, or shrug or anything like that. At least nothing I’ve noticed or that anyone’s pointed out to me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you ever feel a strange sensation or inner pressure that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a strange sensation exactly. It’s more like I get… stuck in the moment.\n\nLike at the register, I’ll start tapping the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I really should be finishing the transaction. It doesn’t really fit what’s happening, I know that. The customer is waiting, and I’m aware of it, but for a second I just don’t move on the way I’m supposed to.\n\nI came in because it’s started causing problems at work. Last week I got written up for it, so… I guess it’s more than just a little habit now. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, brief movements or sounds that you couldn’t control, like eye blinking, nose twitching, or making a noise without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not like blinking or twitching or making sounds without meaning to.\n\nI mean, if I do something like that, I haven’t noticed it, and nobody’s really told me I do. I hope I’m answering that the right way—it’s just not something I recognize in myself.\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you feel any kind of inner pressure, discomfort, or need to do it a certain way before you can move on?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it feels like pressure exactly. It’s not like I’m in pain or anything. It’s more like… I get caught on it, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the register I’ll start tapping the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll just kind of freeze with my hand over the cash drawer, even though the customer is standing there waiting. I know it doesn’t really fit what I’m supposed to be doing. I try to make it seem like I’m just checking something, but it’s been noticeable enough that I got written up last week.\n\nI’m not sure if that’s the kind of “need” you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that you have to perform these tapping patterns or pauses in a specific way to prevent something from happening or to make sure things feel 'just right'?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s to stop something bad from happening, exactly. It’s not like I believe something terrible will happen if I don’t tap the scanner the right number of times.\n\nIt’s more that I get stuck doing it, or I pause when I should be moving on, like with my hand over the cash drawer. I know the customer is waiting, and I know it doesn’t really make sense for the moment. I guess I’m trying to make it look normal, but it’s been enough of a problem at the register that I got written up last week.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s the kind of “just right” feeling you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden, quick movements or sounds that you couldn’t control, like eye blinking, shoulder shrugging, or throat clearing?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, not the shoulder shrugging or throat clearing kind of thing.\n\nIf I’ve done anything like that, it hasn’t been something I noticed or that people have pointed out to me. I don’t want to say the wrong thing, but as far as I know, no.\n\nDoctor: Before you do any of these actions, like tapping the scanner or pausing at the register, do you ever feel a warning or a need in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it right then?\n\nPatient: Not really like a body warning, no. I don’t feel, like, a twitch or a buildup that tells me I have to do it right then.\n\nIt’s more that I just… get stuck. At the register I’ll start tapping the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I should be moving on. I know it doesn’t fit the situation, especially with the customer waiting, and I try to make it look like I’m just checking something. But it’s happened enough that I got written up last week.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you ever feel a small urge or need, like a build-up in your mind or body, that makes you feel like you have to do it right then?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a build-up, exactly. It’s not like a physical urge that comes on first.\n\nIt’s more like I get caught in it at the register—tapping the scanner in a pattern, or sort of freezing with my hand over the cash drawer when I should be finishing the sale. I know the customer is waiting, and I try to make it seem like I’m just doing my job normally, but it doesn’t really fit the moment. It got bad enough that I was written up last week.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s answering what you mean by an urge.\n\nDoctor: Before you start tapping the scanner or pausing the way you do, do you ever feel a small inner tension, discomfort, or need that makes you feel like you have to do it right then?\n\nPatient: I guess… not exactly like a tension in my body. It’s more like I get caught up in it before I realize I’m doing it.\n\nAt the register I’ll start tapping the scanner in this little pattern, or I’ll just pause with my hand over the cash drawer when I should be moving on. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Maybe a little, but I don’t think of it as a clear “need,” if that makes sense. It’s more like I’m at the register and I just get stuck doing something that doesn’t really fit the moment.

Like I’ll tap the scanner in a pattern, or I’ll freeze with my hand over the cash drawer even though the customer is waiting and I know I should be finishing up. I try to make it look normal, like I’m checking something, but… it’s happened enough that I got written up last week.

I’m not trying to be difficult with the question. I just don’t know if what I feel counts as a pull or if it’s more that I realize I’m already doing it.
